股價相對大盤走勢

華邦電 (2344 TT)  Trading Buy

記憶體報價漲幅收斂，建議 Trading Buy 。  
投資建議 ： 4Q26 市場需求呈現結構性分化。 PC 及傳統消費性
電子進入季節性淡季與庫存修正，需求鬆軟； AI 相關產品如 AI
伺服器、 Edge AI 及網基礎建設的需求力道維持強勁，延續至
2027 年，記憶體產業仍呈現供需吃緊，預估華邦電 2026 年和
2027 年稅後 EPS 分別為 23.68 元和 35.90 元。除 AI 伺服器需求
外，交換器及 Edge AI 應用亦帶動 NOR Flash 與 SLC NAND 需
求持續成長，華邦電憑藉 NOR Flash 全球市占率第一及 SLC 
NAND 擴產，可望持續受惠。此外，矽電容目前滿載，新客戶
陸續加入，將成為公司新的獲利來源。雖 2027 年記憶體報價維
持高檔，但消費性電子需求較差，記憶體報價漲幅收斂，影響
華邦電部份業績，因此建議為 Trading Buy ，目標價 162 元。  
華邦電 3Q26 稅後 EPS 7.87 元：記憶體報價上漲， 3Q26 華邦電
合併營收 823.31 億元， QoQ+37.6%(YoY+278.2%) 。CMS 占營收
比重 58% 、 Flash 占營收比重 32% 、 Logic 占 9%，其中 CMS 營
收 QoQ+50% ，成長最明顯。 3Q26 華邦電記憶體營收為 742.84
億元， QoQ+43.7% ， YoY+425.6% 。 Flash 和 CMS 各占記憶體營
收 36% 和 64% 。 3Q26 合併毛利率為  70.57% ，增加約  4.32 個
百分點。 3Q26 記憶體的毛利率從  2Q26 的  70.3% 提升至  
73.9% ， 3Q26 稅後 EPS 7.87 元。  
資本支出 395 億元 ：預估 2026 年資本支出為 395 億元，
YoY+618.18% 。部分原訂於 2026 年底交貨的設備將遞延跨年至
2027 年付款，但整體擴產進度超前，來自製程的轉換。目前台
中廠  DRAM 月產能約 12K 。配合高雄廠  16nm 新產能於 2027
年開出，公司規劃於  2027 年將台中廠  DRAM 月產能降至約
7K。高雄廠  Module A 目前月產能約 15K，預計在 2026 年底前
提升至 24K 並開始新增產能投片。 16nm 月產能目前約 2K。華
邦電已正式啟動高雄廠  Module B 的全新階段擴產計畫，目標
2029 年年中開始裝機，並於  2029 年底前進入量產。

  華邦電 ESG ：  
華邦電堅持所有經營管理活動必須兼顧企業社會責任。經由加強員工教育
訓練及公司內控制度，以確保企業道德及政府法令之遵循。同時，持續不斷
地追求品質管理和環境保護之改善，勠力於社會公益，維持與利害關係人間
之良好溝通。期待所有事業夥伴支持並遵循企業社會責任之要求。

華邦電 3Q26 營收 823.31 億元， QoQ+37.58% ，稅後淨利 354.22 億元，
QoQ+45.67% ，稅後 EPS 7.87 元：  
受惠記憶體報價上漲， 3Q26 華邦電合併營收 823.31 億元，
QoQ+37.6%(YoY+278.2%) 。 CMS( 原 Specialty DRAM 改名客製化記憶體方
案 )占營收比重 58% 、 Flash 占營收比重 32% 、 Logic 占 9%，其中 CMS 營收
QoQ+50% ，成長最明顯。 3Q26 合併毛利率為 70.57% ，增加約 4.32 個百分
點。 3Q26 華邦電記憶體營收為 742.84 億元， QoQ+43.7% ， YoY+425.6% 。
Flash 和 CMS 各占記憶體營收 36% 和 64% 。華邦電 3Q26 合併毛利率為
70.6% ，較 2Q26 的 66.2% 增加約 4 個百分點。 3Q26 記憶體的毛利率從 2Q26
的 70.3% 提升至 73.9% ，產能利用率維持約 100% 的滿載狀態，邏輯 (新唐 )毛

利率則為 37.4% 。  
營業費用方面， 3Q26 營業費用為 143.17 億元 (費用率 18%) ，合併營益率為
53.2% ，歸屬母公司業主淨利為 354.22 億元， QoQ+45.67% ，稅後 EPS 7.87

若單獨以記憶體來看， 3Q26 記憶體產品營收比重方面， Flash 產品線占記憶
體營收比重 36% ，營收 QoQ+34.0% 、 YoY+255% ，位元出貨量 (bit 
shipment)QoQ 持平、 YoY 增加 11~13% ，在 NOR 與 SLC NAND 供給偏緊的
態勢下，推升 ASP(US$)QoQ 增加 31~33% 、 YoY 增加近 200% 。 32nm Flash
占 Flash 營收比重為 17%，24nm 占比達 7%，製程持續轉進； 46nm 占 16%、
45nm Flash 占比為 3%。高容量產品線整體出貨占比持續提升，高容量 NOR 
Flash 營收與出貨占比均創新高，反映 NOR 與 SLC NAND 需求暢旺， 24nm
營收貢獻擴增，受惠於製程技術持續轉換及產品組合優化。  
CMS 占記憶體營收 64%，營收 QoQ+50% 、YoY+620% ，位元出貨量 QoQ 減
少 7~9% 、 YoY 增加 4~6% ，資料中心、 AI 伺服器、 Edge AI 及車用等應用
帶動結構性供給缺口 ， 使得ASP(US$)QoQ 增加近 60%、YoY 增加超過 500%。
3Q26 的 20nm 占 CMS 營收比重達 66%，仍為主要成長動能，主要受惠於 AI
強勁需求，反映 DDR4/LPDDR4 產品線的強勁銷售表現， 16nm 開始放量並
貢獻 3%營收，預期自 2027 年起帶來顯著營收貢獻。  
從產品應用比重來看，通訊電子 (Communication) 占記憶體營收比重 31% ，
QoQ+55% ，受惠於網通等應用需求；車用及工業用相關 (Car & Industrial) 占
營收比重 26% ， QoQ+36% ；消費性電子 (Consumer) 占營收比重 26% ，
QoQ+35% ；電腦相關 (Computer) 占 17%， QoQ+66% 。相關 AI 伺服器、 Edge 
AI、車用與基礎架構應用對高容量記憶體與 Code Storage Flash 的需求持續
強勁。

華邦電將 2026 全年的記憶體資本支出預估，由先前預估的 405 億元小幅下

全年的記憶體資本支出約新台幣 395 億元， YoY+618.18% ，其中生產設備資

4 群益投顧 
本支出約占 95%( 特別是晶圓廠設備 )，以加速增產。儘管目前許多機器設備
交期拉長，部分原訂於 2026 年底交貨的設備將遞延跨年至 2027 年付款，但
整體擴產進度不僅如期，甚至超前，來自製程的轉換。 1Q26~3Q26 已實際支
出新台幣 234 億元。  
目前台中廠 DRAM 月產能約 12K 。配合高雄廠 16nm 新產能於 2027 年開
出，公司規劃於 2027 年將台中廠 DRAM 月產能降至約 7K。高雄廠 Module 
A 目前月產能約 15K ，預計在 2026 年底前提升至 24K 並開始新增產能投
片。 16nm 月產能目前約 2K，現階段良率接近 80% ，目標在 2026 年底前提
升至 90% 以上。長期理想目標是將 16nm 產能拉高至 16K ，逐步取代舊製
程。受惠於 16nm 產能的開出，預估 2027 年高雄廠的位元產出將接近倍增。
高雄廠 Module A 達到 24K 產能後，至 2028 年底前空間已塞滿，因此華邦
電已正式啟動高雄廠 Module B 的全新階段擴產計畫，目標在 2026 年底正
式動工，預計 2029 年年中開始裝機，並於 2029 年底前進入量產。 Module B
的潔淨室面積約 30,000 平方公尺，約為現有 Module A 的兩倍。完整裝滿後
可容納約 50K~60K 的月產能 ， 但公司強調會配合客戶的預測與 LTA( 長期合
約 )採分階段裝機，不會一次裝滿。規劃生產 Standard DRAM 、 CUBE 
DRAM( 客製化記憶體 )、 Wafer-on-Wafer( 晶圓堆疊 )及矽電容 (Si-Cap) 。
Module B 已預留 14nm 、12nm 及 EUV( 極紫外光 )的製程空間。其中 14nm 將
同時開發非 EUV 及 EUV 版本， EUV 版為後續 12nm 做準備。因 EUV 設備
交期長達 3 至 3.5 年，公司必須提前向設備廠規劃。已有客戶開始提前洽談
2029 至 2030 年 Module B 的產能與 LTA 。  
公司先前投入約新台幣 40 億元建立額外的矽電容產線，目前產能已全數售
罄 。 矽電容採用獨立產線配置 ， 因此擴產與裝機完全不會排擠到 16nm DRAM
的產能與設備規劃。由於產能供不應求，公司目前正積極在高雄廠尋找新的
空間以利後續擴產。此外， 11/2026 華邦電董事會通過 730 億資本支出，主
要用於高雄廠新廠 Module B 的建置與裝機費用。  
圖一：華邦電記憶體資本支出

資料來源：華邦電，群益投顧預估整理 
華邦電在矽電容取得 MLCC 廠和 AI 晶片的客戶：  
華邦電的矽電容起初並非獨立規劃的生意，而是華邦電在開發客製化記憶
體 (CUBE) 時，為解決電壓驟降 (voltage drop) 而提供給客戶的保護電路。由

### Page 5

個股報告  
                                        2026 年 11 月 4 日

5 群益投顧 
於產品規格極佳，部分非客製化記憶體的客戶進而主動要求試作，華邦電順
勢將其發展成一項獨立產品。  
隨著 AI、先進封裝 (如 CoWoS 等 )及細微穩壓 (1.2 伏、 0.9 伏以下 )的需求爆
發，矽電容因具備極小、極薄、耐高溫且電容值精準的特性，迅速成為解決
封裝雜訊與穩壓的最佳方案。  
台積電雖為全球最大的矽電容製造商，但其矽電容多整合於自身先進封裝
(如  CoWoS 中介層 )內搭售，不對外單獨銷售。華邦電採取純晶圓代工模式，
提供  PDK ，讓客戶或 Design House 自行設計，由華邦電負責生產與專屬機
台投片。客戶群涵蓋 MLCC 大廠與 AI 晶片廠，應用於光通訊模組、 RF 射
頻模組與  3D 封裝。  
為避免矽電容生產擠壓到利潤更好的 DRAM 產能，華邦電特別為矽電容開
闢獨立的生產專線。公司先前已針對此專線投資約新台幣 40 億元，華邦電
在高雄廠 Module A 設置矽電容專用產線，現有產能約數千片晶圓 /月，前期
開出產能已全數售罄。面對新增客戶少量需求 (如 500 片至 2,000 片 )，目前
採產能彈性調配方式供應。  
技術規格方面，華邦電矽電容的容值達到 5,400nF/mm²(5.4µF/mm²) ，高於同
業之 1,500nF/mm² 或 2,700nF/mm² 。產品厚度約 10µm ，適配於 PCB 埋入、
CoWoS 載板 (Substrate) 設置或晶片堆疊架構，應用涵蓋 AI 加速器、 3D 封
裝、光模組及高頻 RF 領域。全球前幾大 MLCC 廠商透過華邦電晶圓代工
(Foundry) 模式與 PDK 進行設計及投片。  
未來產能擴充規劃方面，高雄廠 Module B 已預留矽電容專用機台與專線空
間，後續將依長期合約 (LTA) 進度開出產能。矽電容製程僅使用 DRAM 前段
約五分之一設備 (製造電容結構 1C，不需製造電晶體結構 1T) ，產能擴充不
排擠 16nm DRAM 之設備與產能配置 。 相較於生產需要耗時 170 天的 DRAM，
矽電容的生產 cycle time 只需要 30 天。在相同的生產時間內， DRAM 跑 1
個生產 cycle ，矽電容則可以跑 5 個 cycle ，這大幅提升設備的周轉率。  
華邦電原本預期矽電容在 2027 年時能占 DRAM 營收的 10% 。然而由於
DRAM 本身的營收分母在近期成長得太大，要達到 10% 的占比變得困難，
目前評估能維持 5%營收占比。不過，矽電容發展的絕對總金額與公司原先
計畫的預期差不多，公司依舊看好其長期的成長動能。 5,400nF/mm² 產品毛
利率接近公司平均水平，  
華邦電宣布將以全現金方式取得 Infineon 的 NOR Flash 與 F-RAM 事業
100% 的股權：  
09/16/2026 華邦電發布重訊，宣布將與德國半導體大廠 Infineon(Infineon) 旗
下的 Infineon Technologies LLC 簽署股份購買協議。華邦電將以全現金約
11.2 億美元 (以匯率 31.6 估算約新台幣 353.92 億元 )，取得 Infineon 
Technologies LLC 旗下 NOR Flash 與 F-RAM 事業經重組後標的公司的 100%
股權。  
交易完成後，華邦電將直接或間接持有標的公司 100% 股權，標的公司並將
重新以「 Spansion 」為名營運 ， 形成Winbond 與 Spansion 雙品牌布局 。Spansion
總部續留美國加州聖荷西 (San Jose) ，保留原 Infineon/Cypress 的研發核心與
歐美技術團隊 (跨十餘國人才 )。

### Page 6

個股報告  
                                        2026 年 11 月 4 日

6 群益投顧 
交易總金額將依 Stock Purchase Agreement 所約定之交割日財務狀況及價格
調整機制結算後確定，資金來源為自有資金或融資活動支應。本交易預計於
2H27 完成，惟仍須取得相關主管機關核准，並達成或依法豁免股份購買協
議所約定之其他交割先決條件。  
此次收購對華邦電而言，則可藉此取得完整 NOR Flash 線，並新增 F-RAM
產品與相關全球客戶 、 研發資源 ， 進一步提高在NOR Flash 市 占 率。依Omdia
統計， 2025 年全球 NOR Flash 市場規模約 34 億美元，其中華邦電市占 23%
居首，其次為兆易創新 (603986 SHA) 及旺宏 (2337 TT) ，各占 17% ， Infineon
占 11% 、排名第四。  
從產品及客戶結構來看，雙方既有客戶重疊程度不高。華邦電 NOR Flash 應
用以 PC、 NB 市場為主， Infineon 相關業務則具有較多歐美級車用客戶，有
助公司收購後直接擴大 NOR Flash 規模，並補足不同區域及終端市場的客
戶布局。  
價格維持高檔，預估華邦電 2026 年和 2027 年稅後 EPS 分別為 23.68 元和
35.90 元：  
華邦電在 AI 邊緣運算方面，提供 CUBE(Customized Ultra Bandwidth
Element) 方案，並參加 UCIe 聯盟，華邦電自主開發高頻寬 CUBE 記憶體，
適用於邊緣 AI 應用， AI、HPC 領域的  3D TSV DRAM 。目前推進進度符合
預期，主要應用於 AI 加速器 (ASIC/XPU) 之推論運算，公司進行 Wafer-on-
Wafer 堆疊四層 DRAM 並整合客戶單層 SoC ，目前內部三層堆疊成功率超
過 99% ，預計於 2H27 開始小量量產。  
CMS 技術發展與產品組合方面 ， 產品線主要聚焦於 DDR4 與 LPDDR4 。20nm
為當前營收大宗，佔 CMS 事業比重約 66% ，公司同時保留部分 20nm 產能
以支應車用、工業及基礎架構 (Infrastructure) 等客戶對 DDR3 的長期需求。
16nm 已開始投片出貨， 3Q26 佔 CMS 比重為 3%。隨著高雄廠 Module A 擴
產至 24K 至 25K/ 月， 16nm 月產能由 2K 提升至 16K ，公司規劃 2027 年下
半年 16nm 佔記憶體營收超過 40% 以擴大位元產出。客製化超高頻寬記憶體
CUBE 專為 AI 加速器 (Inference 、 XPU 、 ASIC) 設計，晶圓堆疊 (Wafer-on-
Wafer) 試疊三層成功率達 99%，預計 2027 年進入量產並提供 SoC 搭配四層
DRAM 之 3D 堆疊架構 。 此外 ， 公司切入企業級SSD(eSSD) 內建 DRAM Cache
供應鏈，規劃 2027 年起於電腦領域擴大貢獻。  
Flash 技術發展與產品組合方面，公司在 512Mb 、 1Gb 及 2Gb 等高容量領域
建立市場地位， 3Q26 高容量 NOR Flash 營收與出貨佔比均創歷史新高，主
力製程包含 58nm 。SLC NAND 專注於程式碼儲存 (Code Storage) 市場， 24nm
出貨比重持續提升 (3Q26 佔 Flash 營收 7%) 並逐步取代 32nm。 產品組合涵蓋
1.2V 、1.8V 、 3V QSPI/Octal NOR 、 Secure NOR 及 SLC NAND/MCP 等方案。
Flash 市場與營運展望方面， NOR Flash 方面， AI 伺服器架構於
Compute/Switch/Network Tray 、 SmartNIC 、 DPU 、 BMC 及 BIOS/UEFI 等區
塊均搭載 NOR Flash ，伺服器應用佔比提升，以 58nm 製程為大宗。市場上
大於等於 512Mb 的高容量 NOR Flash 供給持續吃緊，公司產能往 512Mb 以
上配置，市佔率超過 60% 至 70%；中國廠商產能主要集中在小於等於 32Mb
或 64Mb 的低容量消費性市場 。 單架新世代 AI 伺服器機櫃需搭載 500 至 600

7 群益投顧 
顆 NOR Flash 。華邦內部預估全球伺服器 NOR Flash 需求量於 2027 年達 2.4
億顆 (2025 年至 2028 年年複合成長率為 33%)，其中大於等於 512Mb 高容量
產品將佔 2027 年總需求之 65%。華邦在 AI 生態系之伺服器 NOR 位元出貨
量預估 2026 年 YoY+112% 、2027 年 YoY+67% 。高容量 NOR 價格上行趨勢
預估延續至 2027 年。 SLC NAND 方面，在 2027 年維持正向價格展望。英
飛凌 NOR Flash 與 F-RAM 事業收購案預計於 2H27 完成交割，後續將以
Spansion 品牌維持獨立營運，以深化高階、車用及國防領域布局。  
華邦電表示， 4Q26 市場需求呈現結構性分化。 PC 及傳統消費性電子進入季
節性休養與庫存修正，需求呈現鬆軟態勢； AI 相關產品受 AI 伺服器、 Edge 
AI 及網通基礎建設拉貨帶動，需求力道維持強勁。華邦電藉由 4Q26 非 AI
需求小幅修整，調控部分出貨與產能，保留產能予簽署長期合約 (LTA) 的策
略夥伴使用。  
在價格與合約價走勢方面，客製化記憶體事業 (CMS/DRAM) 於 4Q26 合約價
格持續微幅向上調漲，整體價格維持走揚。快閃記憶體事業 (Flash) 在 AI 伺
服器與車用需求支撐下，高容量 NOR Flash 供給維持緊缺，價格上行趨勢延
續； SLC NAND 受強勁 Design-ins 支撐，價格展望正面。  
產業供需結構方面，全球主要記憶體同業缺乏大幅新增產能，記憶體供不應
求的緊俏態勢延伸至 2027 年至 2028 年。 2027 年記憶體市況評估將較 2026
年更為吃緊 。 預估華邦電2026 年和 2027 年稅後 EPS 分別為 23.68 元和 35.90
元。

  【投資評等說明】  
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間 ≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy) 5% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等  
預期近期股價將處於盤整  
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】  
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。   
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。  
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。  
停止推薦情境：  
1.  達目標價。  
2.  雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。  
3.  推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」 。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

### Page 6

Chroma ATE (2360.TW)
30 October 2026 Citi Research
6
Chroma ATE
Company description
Chroma ATE is a leading supplier of testing and instrumentation equipment,
automated testing systems (ATS), and turnkey solutions. Its products are
used for testing power electronics, semiconductor, IC, EV component,
display, and other electronics applications.
Investment strategy
We rate Chroma Buy as we expect structural and robust growth ahead, driven
by: 1) strong power tester demand for AI data center/servers; 2) expanding SLT

(system level testing) demand driven by new generation model launch and
new customers ramp-up; 3) strong metrology growth from increasing foundry
capex investment in advanced packaging; and 4) solid silicon photonics
equipment demand, with CPO a key incremental upside.
Valuation
Our target price of NT$2,900 is based on 40x 2027E P/E, at close to 1x PEG.
Chroma shares have re-rated above its historical range as its growth outlook
is now strongly tied to AI infrastructure build out. We estimate 60%+ of its

revenue are now AI-related, with strong growth across product segments
including power testers, SLT, metrology, and photonics. Chroma has a
commanding market share in most of these segments and well positioned to
capitalized on the global AI expansion.
Risks
Downside risks to achieving our target price include: 1) softer AI/CPO growth
expectation due to changes in capex outlook or production bottleneck; 2)
fewer SLT needed as a result of shortened cycle time as GPU yield rate

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
In this report, we focus on China's manufacturing PMI, which we
see as a potential catalyst for AirTAC's and Hiwin's share prices.
China's manufacturing PMI fell to 49.3 in October from 50.1 in September:

• This came in lower than both our estimate and consensus of 49.9.
• The production index decreased 1.9ppt while the new order index declined
1.1ppt MoM.
Our view:
• We believe the soft manufacturing PMI data could weigh on market
sentiment in the near term. Nonetheless, we prefer supply players with
company-specific drivers such as Airtac and Hiwin:
° Airtac (1590.TW, OW) should continue to benefit from its own share
gains and incremental contribution from linear guides. Valuation appears

undemanding at 16x 2027e P/E, vs. the 25x average since 2020 (see our
30 Oct 2026 report).
° Hiwin (2049.TW, OW) should show more margin expansion in coming
quarters, supported by higher utilization and price hikes. We see
potential share price upside at the current 28x 2027e P/E, vs. peak cycle
valuation of 35-40x.
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
AirTAC International (1590.TW)
Base case, 29x 2027e P/E. We believe this methodology better captures earnings growth
momentum for industrial automation component plays (market sentiment moves along
with business cycles). As the industry enters an up-cycle, we think applying a near-peak cycle
valuation of 29x P/E is reasonable given the stronger business outlook.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger macroeconomic climate in China

n Stronger-than-expected demand from industrial automation
Risks to Downside
n Sharper economic downturn in China
n Miniature linear guideway business development taking more time
n Larger-than-expected investment in new products but weaker-than-expected demand
Hiwin Technologies Corp. (2049.TW)
Base case, P/E. We think this valuation methodology better captures Hiwin's earnings growth
momentum, as its valuation tends to move with the business cycle. We apply a target multiple

of 38x to our 2027 EPS estimate. We view this multiple as justified by the 47% operating profit
CAGR we project for 2025-28.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger industrial automation demand pickup.
n Market share gains.
n Earlier-than-expected revenue contribution from humanoids.
Risks to Downside
n Weaker industrial automation demand.
n More pricing pressure.
n Market share loss.
n More broad-based impact on the global economy from geopolitical risks.

Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

 절대 (6.6) (10.0) (6.5)

 절대 (달러환산) (5.2) (2.7) 0.2

3Q26 영업이익 1.8조, 핵심 하락 원인은 인센티브 
환율효과는 전분기 대비 -5,330억 작용했으나, 기말환율 하락에 따른 판매보증비의 급감 
(6,200억, -9,250억 qoq)으로 상쇄되었다 판단. 인센티브 비용 증가세는 -6,060억원의 영
향을 미쳤으며, 대부분 유럽지역 EV2 판매를 위한 딜러 인센티브에서 발생 추정. 
외생변수는 능력 밖의 일, EV 전략에 대해서는 장기성장에 무게 
중동 분쟁 지속, 환율 하락과 같은 외생변수의 부정적 영향을 제외한다면 단기 영업적 측면
의 실적 악재는 BEV 인센티브 지급기조의 변화가 없을 것이라는 점. 중장기 글로벌 BEV 
시장의 입지 강화를 단기 전사 수익성보다 중시하는 전략을 채택. 
마찬가지로, 4분기가 환율 효과의 본격화 
3Q26과 반대로 qoq 기말환율 대비 낙폭 확대할 평균환율 (QTD 1,338원) 효과로 환관련 
감익 본격화 예상. 즉, 판매보증비의 정상화 (qoq 반등)로 인해 수익성 추가 악화 가능성 존
재. 완성차 판매량의 경우 경쟁사 대비 견조했던 2Q-3Q26의 기조를 유지할 것이라 예상. 
투자의견 BUY 유지, 목표주가 129,000원으로 하향 
목표주가 하향 근거는 산정 기준을 FY27F로 변경함과 동시에 FY27F 자동차사업 NOPAT 
예상치 하향과 현대모비스 지분가치 하락. 밸류에이션상 저점에 도달했다는 의견은 FY27F 
EPS의 yoy 하락으로 설득력 약화. 중장기 BEV 시장 입지 강화에 대한 밸류에이션 프리미
엄 반영은 글로벌 전동화 속도에 달려 있음. 주가 상승 트리거는 1) 외생변수의 개선, 2) 신
차 출시를 통한 상품성 강화, 3) BEV 시장 점유율 확대 이후 점진적 인센티브 축소, 4) 20조
원 수준의 순현금 기반 발생 가능한 사업적 결정.

## Document 215: [000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 실적에 수급을 더한다SK하이닉스(000660) 실적에 수급을 더한다실적에 수급을 더한다 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 실적에 수급을 더한다SK하이닉스(000660) 실적에 수급을 더한다실적에 수급을 더한다 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-07
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
여전히 가격 상승 구간 
SK하이닉스의 26년 3분기 매출액은 95.8조원, 영업이익 75.1조원으로 예상한다. 
이전 전망 대비 감소한 수준이다. 원/달러 환율 영향이다. 영업이익률은 78.4%로 전 
분기 대비 2.1%p 상승할 전망이다. DRAM 매출액은 73.1조원, 영업이익 
59.2조원으로 추정한다. DRAM ASP는 이전 전망 대비 상향 조정했다. 일부 제품 
가격 인상을 반영한 영향이다. Bit Growth는 이전 전망 수준을 유지했다. ASP 
상향이 수익성 개선에 미친 영향은 크지 않을 전망이다. NAND 매출액은 22.4조원, 
영업이익은 15.9조원으로 예상한다. 
Global Top 수준 주주환원 규모/자사주 살 게 더 있다 
SK하이닉스는 2026년 8월 19일 주주환원에 대한 새로운 정책을 제시했다. FCF에서 
CAPEX를 제외한 금액의 50% 이상을 주주환원에 사용한다는게 핵심이다. 2026년 
기준으로 최소 90조원 수준일 것으로 추정한다. 자사주 중심으로 주주환원을 계획 
중이고 이미 집행 중인 40조원 이외에 4분기 중에 추가로 자사주 매입이 진행될 
것으로 예상한다. 배당도 일부 집행이 되겠지만 자사주가 중심이 될 전망이다. 
흔들리지 말아야 할 이유는 많다 
SK하이닉스에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. AI 모델의 성장으로 메모리에 
대한 수요가 지속적으로 높아지고 있고, 이에 비해 공급 증가는 제한적인 상황이 
상당기간 지속될 것으로 전망하기 때문이다. 이로 인해 현재 수준의 DRAM, NAND 
가격에 변동성은 낮고, 높은 수익성이 유지되면서 안정적 현금흐름 창출이 가능할 
것으로 전망한다. 현재 주가는 지나치게 저평가되어 있는 국면에 있다고 판단한다. 
목표주가는 12개월 예상 EPS 446,092원에 PER 9 배를 적용한 4,000,000원을 
유지한다.  
목표주가 (유지) 4,000,000원  
현재가 (10/6) 1,773,000원

KOSPI (10/6)  6,941.39pt  
시가총액 1,295,163십억원  
발행주식수 730,492천주  
액면가 5,000원

Compliance Notice 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 외부의 압력이나 부당한 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하여 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
동 자료는 기관투자가 또는 제3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다. 
동 자료는 조사분석자료 작성에 참여한 외부인(계열회사 및 그 임직원등)이 없습니다. 
조사분석 담당자 및 배우자는 해당종목과 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 
동 자료에 언급된 종목의 지분율 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
당사는 상기 명시한 사항 외 고지해야 하는 특별한 이해관계가 없습니다.

종목명 담당자 담당자(배우자) 보유여부 1%이상 보유여부 유가증권 발행관련 계열사 관계여부 공개매수 사무취급 IPO 회사채 지급보증 중대한 이해관계 M&A 관련 수량 취득가 취득일 
해당 사항 없음

주가수익률 (%) 1개월 3개월 12개월  
 절대 (24.9) (6.2) (23.5)  
 상대 (13.1) (10.5) (47.4)  
 절대 (달러환산) (23.8) 1.5  (18.0)

3Q26 Review: “조삼모사” Again  
3Q26P 연결 실적은 매출액 6조 943억원(YoY -6.0%), 영업이익 4,520억원(YoY -46.2%, OPM 
7.4%)을 기록하며 컨센서스 대비 연결 매출액(7.5조원)과 영업이익(1.16조원) 모두 대폭 하회했다. 주
요 자회사가 양호한 실적을 기록했음에도 불구하고, 지상방산 실적이 매우 부진했다. 지상방산은 매출
액 1조 2,455억원(YoY -41.0%), 영업이익 217억원(YoY -96.2%, OPM 1.7%)을 기록했는데, 1)3Q 
생산 물량이 4Q 매출 인식되는 흐름 속, 고정비 부담이 컸었고, 2)낮은 수출 비중(3Q 48%, 2Q 58%)
과 국내외 사업의 부진한 믹스(국내 BEP 수준, 해외 OPM 11% 추정), 3)비우호적인 원/달러 환율, 4)
대전 사업장 사고 복구 및 기타 일회성 비용(300억원+160억원, 총 460억원) 등이 반영되었다.   
대전 사업장 정상화 시점과 사업 차질 영향은? 
대전 사업장은 내년 상반기 중 정상화될 예정이며, 보은과 여수 공장에서 생산 보완을 위한 조치가 진
행 중인 것으로 소통했다. 또한 대전 사업장은 국내 물량 위주이며, 수출 납기(천무, 천궁-II 추진체 
등)에는 지장이 없을 것이라고 언급했지만, 기타 사업장 램프업을 위한 인건비 증가 등, 수익성 악화는 
불가피할 것으로 예상된다.  
15%대의 매출 성장 가이던스는 달성이 가능할까? 
보수적으로 접근하는 것이 합리적이라는 판단이다. 당사는 ‘26E 지상방산 매출액을 일부 ‘27~28E로 
이연하면서 -6.1% 하향 조정했다. 사측은 폴란드 EC1 천무 인도 가이던스를 기존 40문에서 60문으
로 상향 조정하는 등 납품 속도가 예정대비 빨라진 것을 언급했다. 다만, 천무 EC1에서 미사일 물량 
비중이 높은 것으로 파악되기 때문에 생산 지연 가능성과 수출입 데이터 상 12월 출하 물량의 매출 인
식 이월 가능성을 반영했다. ‘27E는 폴란드 후속 사업과 루마니아 K9, 호주 레드백 등이 양산 페이즈
에 진입하며 성장세가 지속될 전망이다.    
투자의견 Buy, 목표주가 1,480,000원 유지 
실적 쇼크와는 별개로, 미국 K9MH 시제, 스페인 K9, 크로아티아 천무 등 수주는 서프라이즈라고 판
단된다. 분기말 수주잔고는 35조원(수출 비중 74%, 스페인 미포함)으로 중장기 성장성에 대한 이견은 
없다. 불가피한 분기 실적 변동으로 동사의 미래 가치를 예단하는 것은 시기상조라는 판단이다.

## Document 258: [329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사HD현대중공업(329180) 피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사HD현대중공업(329180) 피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

(329180) 
HD 현대중공업 
2026.10.29

AI로보틱스/방산/조선/운송 양승윤 
6139 / syyang0901@eugenefn.com

피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사 
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 610,000원(하향) 
 현재주가 285,500원(10/28)

 3Q26 매출액은 5조 7,536억원(+30.2%yoy), 영업이익은 8,992억원
(+61.3%yoy, OPM 15.6%)을 기록. 영업이익 컨센서스 9,792억원 하회.  
 평균 환율이 약 79원 하락하며 영업이익 약 450억원이 줄었고, 조업
일수는 47일로 통상 계절성 대비 4일 추가 감소해 약 500억원의 이
익 감소 효과 발생. 임단협 추가 비용의 약 75%도 3분기에 선반영
(잔여분은 4분기 반영). 일회성 요인을 제외하면  이익 체력은 견조. 
 동사의 조선 부문 매출액은 4조 3,988억원(+44.0%yoy), 영업이익은 
6,463억원(+62.8%yoy, OPM 14.7%)을 기록. 상선 OPM은 14.7%로 
2Q26(17.8%) 대비 하락했으나 대부분 외생변수 영향. 3Q26 대형선 매출 
중 2023년 수주분 비중은 45%, 2024~2025년 수주분은 51%. 고선가 
물량의 매출 비중이 높아지는 2027년까지 수익성 개선 여력은 충분.  
 9월 말 누적 상선 수주는 138.0억달러로 연간 목표의 120%, 엔진기계는 
41.4억달러로 155%를 달성했으나 함정(13%)과 해양(5%)은 부진. LNG선
은 중국 조선소의 저가 수주와 발주 둔화로 선가가 일부 하락. 선주들이 현 
선가를 저점으로 인식하며 발주 협의를 재개하는 분위기. 북미 LNG 프로젝
트 FID가 이어지고 있어 2027년 LNG선 발주 회복 기대. 
 함정 부문은 매출 2,570억원(-14.6%qoq)으로 줄었으나, 수출 비중 확대에 
따른 믹스 개선으로 영업이익 386억원(+114%qoq, OPM 15.0%)을 기록. 
해양플랜트는 매출액 3,799억원(+35.3%yoy), 영업이익 1,265억원(OPM 
33.3%). C/O 180억원을 제외해도 Ruya 공정 본격화와 Trion 고수익 물량
으로 높은 수익성 유지. 2027년 일부 프로젝트 인도로 매출 감소가 예상되
나, LNG선·블록 물량 투입으로 고정비 부담을 완화할 계획. 신규 해상변전
소(OSS) 계약을 앞두고 있으며, 2027년 초 추가 수주 발표도 기대. 
 주목할 부분은 엔진기계. 영업이익 2,299억원(OPM 24.2%)으로 높은 D/F 
엔진 비중을 바탕으로 견조한 수익성 유지. 육상발전용 힘센엔진은 올해   
3GW를 확보하며 2029년까지 계획 캐파 수준의 물량을 이미 채움. 생산 
예상 물량은 2028년 약 1.5GW, 2029년 약 3GW이며, 신규 공장 구축 이
후 연간 생산능력은 약 4GW로 확대 예정. 북미 Hyperscaler와의 직접 협
상이 늘고 있으며, 힘센엔진을 활용한 부유식 발전설비(BMPP)로 FDC 시장
까지 대응 가능. AI 데이터센터 전력 수요가 2028년 이후 새로운 이익 축. 
 미국에서는 Kiewit과 합작법인 설립 계약 체결. 군수지원함 등 지원함 사업
부터 추진하며, 현지 조선소 건설 과정에서 HII에 선박 블록을 공급하는 방
안도 협의 중. 구체적인 투자계획은 2026년 말까지 확정 목표. 국내에서는 
핵추진잠수함 초기 예산이 2027년 반영되며 동사도 사업 참여를 준비 중. 
 동사에 대한 투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 61만원을 제시. 고선가 물량 매출 확
대와 육상발전용 엔진 성장으로 피크아웃을 이겨낼 굴지의 조선사.

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

투자기간 및 투자등급/투자의견 비율

Source PDF: all report pdf/[373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) ESS 수주 모멘텀 이어진다LG에너지솔루션(373220) ESS 수주 모멘텀 이어진다ESS 수주 모멘텀 이어진다 - 기업.pdf

### Page 1

2026.11.04   
LG에너지솔루션 
(373220)

ESS 수주 모멘텀 이어진다 Company Brief

3Q26 실적, 원-달러 환율 하락에도 시장 기대치 대폭 상회 
동사의 3Q26 실적은 매출 9.6조원(+59% YoY, + 28% QoQ), 영업이익 7,560억원 
(+26% YoY, +568% QoQ)으로 원/달러 환율 하락에도 불구하고 시장기대치(매출 
8.8조원, 영업이익 3,217억원)를 크게 상회했다. 당사는 Stellantis와의 캐나다 
합작관계 종료에 따른 보상금 등을 포함해 약 4,250억원의 일회성 이익이 반영된 
것으로 추정한다. 이를 제외한 영업이익은 약 3,310억원으로, 시장기대치에 대체로 
부합한 것으로 판단된다. AMPC는 미국 ESS 생산량 증가와 GM 얼티엄셀즈 재가동에 
힘입어 전분기 대비 약 73% 증가한 4,169억원을 기록했다. 사업부문별로는 ESS의 
실적 개선이 두드러졌다. 상반기 배터리 패키징 병목으로 이연됐던 물량 출하가 
집중되면서 ESS 출하량은 전분기 대비 60% 가량 증가한 것으로 추정된다. 자동차 
전지도 유럽향 물량 회복과 GM 얼티엄셀즈 재가동으로 매출이 전분기 대비 약 
33%로 증가했으며, 고정비 부담 완화에 따라 적자폭도 뚜렷하게 축소된 것으로 
판단된다. 소형전지는 주요 고객사인 Tesla의 판매 호조에 따른 출하 증가로 매출 
회복과 수익성을 뒷받침한 것으로 보인다. 
4Q26 ESS 중심의 수익성 개선과 수주 모멘텀 지속될 전망 
미국 ESS 시장은 신재생 에너지 확대와 AI 데이터센터의 전력 수요 증가에 힘입어 
빠르게 성장하고 있다. 특히 정책 환경도 비PFE 공급망을 확보한 업체에 유리하게 
작용하고 있다. 동사는 3Q26 실적 컨퍼런스 콜에서 ESS 수주잔고가 약 
160GWh이며, 2028 년까지 캐파의 대부분에 대응하는 물량을 확보했다고 밝혔다. 
또한 현재 다수의 고객사들과 2028년 이후 공급 물량을 협의하고 있어 연말까지 
추가적인 수주 모멘텀이 이어질 것으로 예상된다. 이를 바탕으로 4Q26에도 동사의 
본업 실적은 ESS 중심의 개선세를 이어갈 전망이다. 전분기에 반영된 대규모 일회성 
이익의 기저효과로 영업이익은 감소하겠지만, 이를 제외하면 출하량 증가와 가동률 
상승에 따른 점진적인 수익성 개선이 예상된다. 수익성 중심의 사업 효율화를 위해 
소형 IT용 파우치 사업을 축소하고 있다는 점도 중장기 손익 개선에 긍정적이다. 이를 
반영한 4Q26 매출과 영업이익은 각각 약 10.5조원(+63% YoY, +9% QoQ), 
4,150억원(흑자전환 YoY, -45% QoQ)으로 추정된다. 
매수 투자의견과 목표주가 유지 
동사에 대한 매수 투자의견과 목표주가를 유지한다. 북미 ESS 수요 증가와 공급망 
재편에 따른 신규 수주 확대가 중장기 실적 성장을 뒷받침할 전망이다. 특히 ESS용 
생산라인 전환과 가동률 상승이 본격화되면 매출 증가에 더해 고정비 부담 완화와 
AMPC 수혜 확대가 맞물리면서 수익성도 개선될 것으로 예상된다. ESS 의 역할이 
신재생 에너지 연계용 저장장치를 넘어 AI 데이터센터의 안정적인 전력 공급을 
지원하는 핵심 인프라로 확대되고 있다는 점에도 주목할 필요가 있다. 이에 따라 
동사의 밸류에이션 역시 기존 EV 중심의 평가에서 벗어나 전력 인프라 산업의 
성장성을 반영하는 방향으로 재평가되어야 한다. 업종 내 실적 개선 가시성과 정책 
수혜가 뚜렷하고, 4Q26 신규 수주에 따른 주가 상승 가능성도 높다는 점에서 동사에 
대한 비중 확대 전략이 유효하다는 판단이다. 
Buy (Maintain)

 회사는 해당 종목을 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 금융투자분석사와 그 배우자는 해당 기업의 주식을 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 당 보고서는 기관투자가 및 제 3자에게 E-mail등을 통하여 사전에 배포된 사실이 없습니다.  
 회사는 6개월간 해당 기업의 유가증권 발행과 관련 주관사로 참여하지 않았습니다. 
 당 보고서에 게재된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

[투자의견] 
종목추천 투자등급  
종목투자의견은 향후 12개월간 추천일 종가대비 해당종목의 예상 목표수익률을 의미함.  
 Buy(매수): 추천일 종가대비 +15% 이상  
 Hold(보유): 추천일 종가대비 -15% ~ 15% 내외 등락 
 Sell(매도): 추천일 종가대비 -15% 이상 
산업추천 투자등급

## Document 279: AI抛售潮血洗亚洲富豪榜，孙正义、三星家族身家遭重创！

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI抛售潮血洗亚洲富豪榜，孙正义、三星家族身家遭重创！.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-06
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

据彭博亿万富翁指数，亚洲 22 位顶级富豪⼀⽇财富缩⽔逾 180 亿美元（约合⼈⺠币 1200 亿
元），软银创始⼈孙正义损失 39 亿美元，三星李在镕家族亦遭重创，合计财富缩⽔约 25 亿美
元。
摘要：
全球科技股抛售潮向亚洲蔓延，重创 AI 产业链核⼼企业，令亚洲顶级富豪财富在⼀⽇之内⼤幅缩
⽔。
据彭博亿万富翁指数，截⾄⾹港时间周五上午 10 时，来⾃⽇本、韩国等国家和地区、跻⾝全球
500 ⼤富豪榜的 22 位亿万富翁合计财富蒸发逾 210 亿美元，此后跌幅有所收窄，最终缩⽔逾 180
亿美元（约合⼈⺠币 1200 亿元）。
其中，软银集团创始⼈孙正义（ Masayoshi Son ）损失最为惨重，个⼈财富单⽇缩⽔ 39 亿美
元，降⾄约 290 亿美元。此轮抛售潮紧随纳斯达克 100 指数创纪录的 2.3 万亿美元市值蒸发之后，
并进⼀步加剧了英伟达 CEO ⻩仁勋跌出全球⼗⼤富豪的财富逆转态势。
市场动荡的导⽕索在于，外界预期⺠主党同时掌控众议院与参议院后，将推动放缓数据中⼼建

设 —— 这⼀ AI 基础设施的核⼼环节 —— 从⽽拖累亚洲科技股全线⾛低。与此同时，据知情⼈⼠透
露，此轮波动还令台湾⾄⾹港⼀带券商的富裕客⼾⾯临杠杆仓位的追加保证⾦压⼒。
孙正义领衔， AI 产业链富豪⾸当其冲
此次财富缩⽔最集中于 AI 产业链核⼼企业的相关股东。
单⽇损失逾 10 亿美元的富豪包括：电⼦传感器制造商基恩⼠（ Keyence Corp. ）的滝崎武光，以
及新加坡⾸富、半导体封测企业⽇⽉光投控（ ASE Technology Holding Co. ）的张虔⽣ (Jason
Chang) 。
孙正义以 39 亿美元（约合⼈⺠币 260 亿元）的单⽇损失位居榜⾸，其财富降⾄约 290 亿美元。软
银集团旗下持有⼤量科技及 AI 相关资产，使其财富⾛势与全球科技股⾏情⾼度绑定。
三星家族集体受损，韩国科技股承压
韩国⽅⾯，三星电⼦⼤股东李在镕及其⺟亲、姐妹 —— 亚洲最富有的家族之⼀ —— 合计财富缩
⽔约 25 亿美元，成为此轮抛售潮中损失最为集中的家族群体之⼀。
三星电⼦作为全球半导体产业的核⼼企业，其股价⾛势直接牵动李⽒家族的财富规模，此次跌

势亦折射出市场对 AI 芯⽚需求前景的重新审视。
对冲基⾦同步受压，杠杆资⾦⾯临追保
此轮市场动荡的冲击范围不仅限于亚洲富豪群体。
据彭博报道， Tiger Global Management 及 Whale Rock Capital Management 等对冲基⾦上⽉已
因 AI 投资前景的不确定性⽽遭受冲击。

## Document 283: AI资本开支“见顶”，美国经济也随之“见顶”？

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI资本开支“见顶”，美国经济也随之“见顶”？.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-07
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

AI 资本开⽀正逼近历史拐点。杰富瑞警告， AI 投资已占美国 GDP 增速的 67% ，⼀旦收缩将
重创经济；⽽信⽤利差扩⼤、半导体持续下跌、超⼤规模云商⾃由现⾦流转负，均已发出
明确⻅顶信号。更深的隐忧在于：这场循环融资体系膨胀⾄ 2.5 万亿美元的 AI 狂潮，或
已 " ⼤⽽不能倒 " 。
摘要：
AI 资本⽀出正逼近历史拐点，⽽美国经济增⻓的命运或与之深度绑定。
据追⻛交易台，杰富瑞策略师 Christopher Wood 在最新报告中警告，⾦融市场已通过信⽤利差
扩⼤和半导体股票持续下跌，发出 AI 资本开⽀周期可能触顶的明确信号 —— 这⼀判断的宏观含义
远不⽌于科技板块本⾝。
据杰富瑞报告，在截⾄ 2026 年三季度的四个季度⾥，美国 AI 相关实际资本⽀出对实际 GDP 同⽐
增⻓的贡献⾼达 1.39 个百分点，占整体 2.1% 增速的 67% 。与此同时，信息处理设备与软件投资
占名义 GDP 的⽐例已升⾄ 5.35% ，超越 2000 年互联⽹泡沫顶峰时期的 4.46% ，创下历史新⾼。

这意味着，⼀旦 AI 资本开⽀出现实质性收缩，美国经济增⻓将⾯临直接且显著的下⾏冲击。
市场已率先作出反应。半导体股票持续承压，信⽤市场出现明显裂缝： Amazon 、 Alphabet 和
Meta 的 10 年期企业债利差较近期低点分别扩⼤ 16 个、 16 个和 35 个基点； Oracle 和 CoreWeave
的 5 年期 CDS 利差则从 6 ⽉初低点分别飙升⾄ 329 个和 1163 个基点。与此同时，美国中期选举后
⺠主党重新掌控国会，市场担忧其对数据中⼼及 AI 模型的监管⽴场将进⼀步压制 AI ⽀出预期。
信⽤市场拉响警报， AI 开⽀共识预测⾯临下修压⼒
信⽤市场的动荡是本轮 AI 资本开⽀⻅顶论最直接的市场依据。杰富瑞报告指出，评估 AI 资本开⽀
⻛险的最佳⽅式，是持续追踪 AI 相关信⽤利差 —— ⽽当前的利差⾛势已发出明确的收紧信号。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

具体数据显⽰， Amazon 、 Alphabet 和 Meta 的 10 年期企业债收益率利差，已从 10 ⽉ 12 ⽇的近期
低点 73 个、 65 个和 94 个基点，分别升⾄ 89 个、 81 个和 129 个基点。 Oracle 和 CoreWeave 的 5 年
期 CDS 利差则从 6 ⽉初低点 153 个和 452 个基点，在 10 ⽉下旬分别飙升⾄ 329 个和 1163 个基点的
⾼位。 SoftBank 的 5 年期 CDS 利差也从 6 ⽉底低点 278 个基点升⾄ 442 个基点。
这⼀信号的核⼼含义在于：市场正在对 AI 相关投资表现出⽇益明显的融资意愿下降。杰富瑞报告
认为，这意味着四⼤超⼤规模云服务商（微软、亚⻢逊、 Alphabet 和 Meta ） 2026 年合计资本开
⽀的市场共识预测 —— 约 1.04 万亿美元 —— 存在实质性下修⻛险。
超⼤规模云商⾃由现⾦流告急， AI 蚕⻝传统业务
最新⼀季财报进⼀步加剧了市场对 AI 资本开⽀可持续性的疑虑。四⼤超⼤规模云服务商三季度合

计资本开⽀同⽐增⻓ 108% ⾄ 2020 亿美元，但与此同时， Amazon 和 Alphabet 的⾃由现⾦流已分
别转负⾄ -350 亿美元和 -90 亿美元。
更令市场警觉的是， AI 正开始蚕⻝超⼤规模云商的传统核⼼业务。Alphabet 的⾕歌搜索收⼊增
速从 2026 年⼀季度的 19% 同⽐⼤幅放缓⾄三季度的 12% ，搜索收⼊占 Alphabet 总营收的⽐例⾸
次跌破 50% ，降⾄ 49.7% ；与此同时，⾕歌云收⼊同⽐暴增 103% ⾄ 307 亿美元，占⽐从 20.7%
升⾄ 24.1% 。 Amazon 的在线商店收⼊增速则从⼆季度的 15% 同⽐骤降⾄三季度的 2% ，⽽ AWS
收⼊同⽐增⻓ 39% ⾄ 460 亿美元。
杰富瑞报告认为，超⼤规模云商⼤规模投⼊ AI 的核⼼动机，恰恰是对 AI 颠覆⾃⾝传统业务的恐惧
—— 这是⼀种防御性⽽⾮进攻性的⽀出逻辑，使其陷⼊ " 不花钱等死，花了钱也未必活 " 的囚徒困
境。
循环融资体系规模膨胀， “ ⼤⽽不能倒 ” ⻛险隐现

杰富瑞报告特别点出美国 AI ⽣态系统中⼀个⽇益庞⼤的结构性⻛险：围绕 AI 资本开⽀构建的循环
融资体系。
四⼤超⼤规模云服务商（微软、亚⻢逊、 Alphabet 和 Oracle ）的剩余履约义务（ RPO ），即合同
收⼊积压，已在上季度末升⾄ 2.5 万亿美元。与此同时，数据中⼼融资正⼤量以表外形式进⾏，
超⼤规模云商与私⼈信贷合作设⽴ " 特殊⽬的 " 投资载体。
CoreWeave 于今年 5 ⽉完成了 31 亿美元的延迟提款定期贷款（ DDTL 5.0 ）融资，这是迄今已知
⾸个通过融资⼦公司公开银团发⾏的 GPU 抵押融资⼯具，其到期收益率已从 7.62% 升⾄ 8.36% 。
据估计，数据中⼼资产证券化的年度新发⾏规模 2025 年已达 270 亿美元， 2026 年预计将升⾄ 400
亿美元。

### Page 3

杰富瑞报告直⾔，上述体系在财务上⻛险极⾼，除⾮假设美国 AI ⽣态系统的部分环节已被视
为 " ⼤⽽不能倒 " 。
微软裁员与中国 AI 降本，预⽰⾏业范式转变
AI 资本开⽀周期的潜在拐点，已在企业⾏为层⾯留下印记。微软宣布向约 6 万名符合条件的员⼯
提供⾃愿离职⽅案，涉及⼈数超过其全球员⼯总数的四分之⼀。杰富瑞报告认为，此举的直接
动因是在资本开⽀账单持续膨胀的压⼒下削减成本 —— 尽管微软三季度⾃由现⾦流仍为正值 186
亿美元。
与此同时，杰富瑞全球量化策略主管 Desh Peramunetilleke 在 10 ⽉中旬的报告中指出，全球 AI
股票的市场⼀致盈利预期增速，预计将从 2026 年的约 100% ⼤幅放缓⾄ 2027 年的 30% 和 2028 年
的 22% ，建议投资者开始分散布局 AI 以外的领域。
在中国，降本逻辑从 AI 热潮伊始便贯穿始终。杰富瑞中国科技与电信研究主管 Edison Lee 指
出，中国 AI 实验室始终聚焦于算⼒效率，这既源于算⼒稀缺，也源于中国 AI 从⼀开始便以商业化
落地应⽤为导向，⽽⾮追求 AGI 这⼀硅⾕式的终极⽬标。这⼀务实路径，或将成为美国 AI ⾏业在
融资收紧压⼒下被迫效仿的⽅向。
若 AI 开⽀急刹⻋，美债难充 " 避险港 "
杰富瑞报告对 AI 资本开⽀急剧收缩的宏观后果作出明确警⽰：这将对美国经济增⻓构成直接负⾯
冲击，并触发美联储政策预期从紧缩向宽松的急速逆转。
然⽽，报告同时指出，在这⼀情景下，美国国债将⽆法扮演传统的 “ 避险资产 ” ⻆⾊。原因在于
“ 财政主导 ” 效应 —— 美国当前财政⾚字已达 GDP 的 6.2% ，⽽历史规律显⽰，经济衰退通常会使
财政⾚字扩⼤约 6 个百分点。⼀旦经济下⾏，市场将迅速将注意⼒转向财政收⼊急剧恶化的前
景，届时美债市场的表现将更接近于⼀个存在问题的新兴市场，⽽⾮传统意义上的安全资产。
报告还援引 Amara 定律提⽰投资者：新技术的短期影响往往被⾼估，⻓期影响则被低估。当前的
市场⾛势表明，全球对 AI 的短期狂热或已触及顶峰。对于投资者⽽⾔，在 AI 资本开⽀周期的不确
定性加剧之际，⻩⾦及⽣物科技等与 AI 资本开⽀相关性较低的资产，或值得给予更多关注。

## Document 310: CEE & CCA week ahead_ Polish GDP and regional inflation data

Source PDF: all report pdf/CEE & CCA week ahead_ Polish GDP and regional inflation data.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-06
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Poland will release third-quarter GDP, ﬁnal inﬂation and current
account data next week, while Hungary publishes September
inﬂation ﬁgures

Adam Antoniak and Peter Virovacz
Polish GDP growth looks set to lose some momentum
Poland: GDP, inﬂation and current account
data due on Friday
Friday brings the release of the 3Q26 GDP ﬂash estimate, ﬁnal
October CPI and September current account data. According to our
estimates, GDP growth moderated to 3.4% YoY from 4.1% YoY in
2Q26. Although the GDP breakdown will not be published until the
end of the month, we forecast that the slowdown in economic
growth reﬂected softer consumption, as higher fuel prices bit into
households’ spending power. We also expect a negative
contribution from net exports, compared with a positive
contribution in 2Q26. Investment activity most likely remained
buoyant.

### Page 2

The ﬁnal October CPI reading should conﬁrm that headline inﬂation
stood at the upper bound of the range around the central bank’s
2.5% target (+/- 1 percentage point). More important than the
headline ﬁgure, however, is the composition of price growth. We
estimate that core inﬂation excluding food and energy prices
moderated to below 3% YoY for the ﬁrst time since March this year.
This supports the National Bank of Poland’s patient approach to
adjusting monetary policy.

The current account deﬁcit probably widened to 1.5% of GDP on a
12-month rolling basis after September, from 1.4% of GDP after
August. We forecast a September deﬁcit of €2380mn, compared
with a negative gap of €932mn in September last year. Foreign
trade continued to expand at a double-digit pace for the fourth
consecutive month, with exports of goods rising by nearly 12% YoY
(in € terms) and imports of goods up by nearly 15% YoY. If oﬃcial
data conﬁrm our estimates, this would imply that net exports

probably made a negative contribution to GDP growth.
Hungary: October inﬂation likely to have
risen on fuel prices and clothing
The spotlight will be on inﬂation in Hungary next week, with
October data due on Tuesday. We are still waiting for the second-
round eﬀects to show up in the data, and we believe the waiting
game will continue. We expect monthly inﬂation of 0.4% in October,
driven mainly by fuel prices and clothing, with the latter largely

reﬂecting seasonal factors.
Services inﬂation is likely to remain muted on a monthly basis,
providing some comfort for monetary policy, while core inﬂation
should remain broadly stable at around 2% year-on-year. Against
this backdrop, the interest rate outlook remains driven primarily by
geopolitics rather than domestic factors.
Key events in CEE & CCA next week

## Document 320: Could Midterms Give Wall Street A Cold Shower_

Source PDF: all report pdf/Could Midterms Give Wall Street A Cold Shower_.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-11-03
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: Could Midterms Give Wall Street A Cold Shower?
Date:    1 hour ago
Could Midterms Give Wall Street A Cold Shower?

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
Could Midterms Give Wall Street
A Cold Shower?
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 2 Nov 2026
View in browser

I. When Gridlock Isn't Good for Stocks
The US midterm elections will be over tomorrow night. According to
betting markets, Democrats have roughly a 95% probability of winning the
House and a 76% probability of taking the Senate (charts).
We suspect that stock investors have yet to fully discount the possibility of
a Democratic sweep of Congress. Some may remain skeptical that polls
confirming strong support for Democrats are accurate. They might reckon

### Page 2

that Democrats are more likely than Republicans to talk to pollsters over
the phone.
In any event, Wall Street traditionally welcomes political gridlock because
it reduces the likelihood that Congress will pass extremist legislation. But
this time could be different for two reasons.
First, deepening partisanship could turn gridlock into damaging political
paralysis at a time when the Bond Vigilantes are already uneasy about
Washington's unsustainable fiscal trajectory. A Democratic sweep could

complicate negotiations over government spending and the debt ceiling.
Prolonged shutdowns and fiscal standoffs could push Treasury yields
higher, weighing on equity valuations.

### Page 3

Second, gridlock's traditional ability to constrain policymaking may not
hold this time. President Donald Trump has relied heavily on executive
authority, with some of his most market-disruptive policies, including tariffs
and military action against Iran, pursued largely without congressional
approval. A Democratic-controlled Congress may therefore do little to
restrain him. Moreover, with the midterms behind him, Trump may feel
even less constrained politically and more willing to pursue controversial

policies through executive action. Hence, a blue wave could deliver a cold
shower for stocks, though we would view a significant selloff as a buying
opportunity given the remarkable strength of the economy and corporate
earnings.
One encouraging development ahead of the elections was today's reports
that the US and Iran are working on a peaceful diplomatic solution to their
war. Iranian President Masoud Pezeshkian said he has granted his
government greater authority to pursue peace talks, while President

Trump and Vice President Vance also signaled progress. Hopes for a deal
sent the price of Brent crude oil down 7.6% to $104.77 per barrel (chart).
However, we have been here before with apparent diplomatic
breakthroughs. We still see a high risk of military re-escalation. The bid-
ask spread remains wide, with Tehran demanding sanctions relief, the
release of frozen assets, and an end to the US naval blockade of Iranian
ports, while Washington insists on nuclear concessions. Meanwhile, Iran's

leverage over the Strait of Hormuz has been weakening. According to
shipping data, for roughly half of September, Gulf oil exporters shipped
more crude than they were shipping before the war.

### Page 4

II. US Economy Continues To Roar
The US economy remains strong despite the volatility of the domestic
political and geopolitical scenes. Consider the following:
(1) Construction spending. Construction spending fell 1.9% m/m in
September to $2.19 trillion (saar), reversing most of August's upwardly
revised 2.0% increase. Nevertheless, spending remains just below last
year's record high (chart).
September's decline was driven by a 5.5% m/m drop in private residential

construction, attributable to a 13.0% plunge in home improvements
(chart). Elevated materials costs and higher mortgage rates continue to
weigh on the housing sector. Meanwhile, private nonresidential
construction rose 1.1% m/m, supported by continued investment in data
centers and power infrastructure.

### Page 5

The AI investment boom remains a powerful tailwind. Data center
construction spending surged 8.6% m/m and 90% y/y in September,
reaching a record $96.5 billion annual rate (chart).
(2) Manufacturing. The ISM M-PMI rose from 54.5 in September to 55.5
in October, marking its tenth consecutive month of expansion, the longest
streak since 2022. Production jumped to the highest reading since
November 2021, while new orders edged up to 55.5. Employment also

remained in expansionary territory (chart).

### Page 6

Inflationary pressures remain troublesome. The prices-paid index eased
from 77.9 to 75.2 in October but remained above 70 for the ninth
consecutive month. Regional Fed surveys also point to persistent price
pressures. Higher energy costs, tariffs, and elevated metals prices
continue to boost input costs (chart).
(3) Bank lending. The Fed's latest Senior Loan Officer Opinion Survey
(SLOOS) painted a mixed picture of credit demand. Banks reported
weaker demand for commercial and industrial (C&I) loans, mortgages,

and auto loans in Q4, while credit-card demand strengthened (chart).

### Page 7

Lending standards remained broadly unchanged for C&I loans but
tightened somewhat for commercial real estate loans (chart).
Encouragingly, actual bank lending tells a happier story. C&I loans surged
11.3% y/y through October 21, while total loans and leases increased
7.9% y/y (chart).

### Page 8

Credit growth is broad across bank sizes, with lending up 8.1% y/y at
large banks and 5.4% at small banks (chart).
Meanwhile, banks' allowances for loan and lease losses have remained
relatively stable at around $202 billion, despite the acceleration in lending
(chart).

### Page 9

clause.
Yardeni QuickTakes © 2026 – Unsubscribe

## Document 396: Preview of 2026 New Zealand General Election (2026-11-07)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Preview of 2026 New Zealand General Election (2026-11-07).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-07
Pages: 13
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1
Westpac New Zealand Economics
• Polling points to a very close and unusually
fragmented election, with the outcome
likely to depend on the performance of the
Opportunity Party and whether any party
achieves “overhang” seats.
• While either Labour or National will lead the
next government, minor parties are likely to
have unprecedented representation in the next
government. This makes it harder to assess
the mix of policies that will emerge from

coalition negotiations.
• Compared to the status quo, a returned
National-led government with stronger
ACT and NZ First representation may be
more aggressive on some combination
of expenditure restraint, welfare and
immigration, resource development
and deregulation.
• A Labour-Green-Opportunity administration
would probably deliver more public-service
and social investment, a broader tax base,
stronger climate and labour regulation and
likely greater borrowing for infrastructure.

A centre-left government may find it hard to
resist pressure to lift public service salaries
and the minimum wage in response to fuel-
driven inflation.
• The fiscal starting point acts as a constraint:
the PREFU projects that OBEGALx will remain
in deficit until 2028/29 and achieving that
outcome will require exceptional spending
control in the current environment.
• Whichever government is elected will need
to make hard trade-offs, or raise additional

revenue or debt. The latter will be costly with
global investors requiring higher interest rates
to fund government (and other) debt.
• A key non-fiscal outcome of a change
in government would likely be the
implementation of the Labour Party’s
proposal to restore the RBNZ’s dual inflation-
employment mandate.
The election appears close, but the bigger
story is fragmentation.
New Zealand goes to the ballot box on 7 November
(although advance voting started on 26 October). The

campaign is taking place against a mixed economic
backdrop: activity has recovered somewhat, but
households remain sensitive to high living costs and a
weak housing market; the fiscal accounts have improved
relative to the Budget but still show deficits until 2028/29.
That combination gives the various parties room to
argue about priorities, but little room to avoid difficult
trade-offs. Additional spending – which may be difficult
to avoid in the current environment – will require either

additional revenue or a willingness to accept additional
debt issuance at a time when international investors are
seeking greater compensation to fund government debt
(and other).
The striking feature of this campaign is the reduced
dominance of the two major parties. Across the major
polls, neither traditional centre-left or centre-right
blocs can be confident of governing alone, and the
composition of the minor-party vote will determine both
who governs and how. Because the Opportunity Party

sits near the threshold, a movement of only a few tenths
of a percentage point could alter the allocation of several
seats. In addition, any “overhang” created by Te Pāti Māori
or the National Party winning more electorates than their
party vote entitlement could also move the majority line
above the usual 61 seats.
ECONOMIC BULLETIN
Preview of 2026 General Election.
27 Oct 2026 |  Westpac Economics Team | westpac.co.nz/economics | economics@westpac.co.nz

Kelly Eckhold, Chief Economist | +64 9 348 9382 | +64 21 786 758 | kelly.eckhold@westpac.co.nz

### Page 2

2
Westpac New Zealand Economics
Together with uncertainty about voter turnout –
especially considering changes in voter registration rules
– the outcome of this General Election is difficult to call.
With major-party support unusually low and the minor
parties offering distinct, sometimes internally conflicting
programmes, the shape of the next government will be
determined not only at the ballot box on 7 November,
but at the negotiating table afterwards. Moreover, if the

results are especially tight, the outcome may not be clear
until sometime after the official results are declared on
27 November. And a hung parliament – where neither the
centre-left nor centre-right bloc can command a majority
– cannot be entirely ruled out either.
How New Zealand’s electoral system works.
New Zealand’s electoral system uses Mixed Member
Proportional representation (or MMP). Voters receive
two votes: an electorate vote for a local candidate and a

party vote for a political party. The electorate candidate
with the most votes wins the local seat. The party vote is
the main determinant of each qualifying party’s overall
representation in Parliament.
Parliament normally has 120 seats. For the 2026
election there are 71 electorates, comprising 64 general
electorates and seven Māori electorates, with the
remaining seats ordinarily filled from party lists. A party
qualifies for proportional representation by receiving

at least 5% of the nationwide party vote or winning an
electorate seat. List MPs are then added to electorate
MPs so that each qualifying party’s total representation
broadly matches its party-vote share.
An overhang arises when a party wins more electorate
seats than its party vote would otherwise entitle it to.
The party keeps those seats and Parliament expands,
which can raise the number required for a majority.
Government formation is separate from the allocation of

seats: a grouping must demonstrate that it can command
confidence in the House and pass supply. That can be
achieved through a formal coalition, a confidence-and-
supply agreement, or other support arrangements. The
largest party is not automatically guaranteed office and
minority governments are possible (e.g. although the
National Party secured more than 44% of the vote in the
2017 election, a minority Labour Party-NZ First coalition
was formed, supported by a confidence-and-supply

arrangement with the Green Party).
The overhang risk is unusually important in 2026 because
current polling leaves both blocs close to, but often
short of, a majority. In addition, the fragmentation of
the electorate increases the risk that one of the major
parties wins more electorates than implied by its party-
vote entitlement. This is in addition to the likelihood that
Te Pāti Māori will achieve an overhang given that it is
running an “electorate vote only” campaign and so asking

its supporters to cast their party votes for other left-
leaning parties. Each additional overhang seat expands
Parliament and so lifts the threshold needed to command
a majority.
The direction of the effect depends on which party
creates the overhang. Extra Te Pāti Māori electorate seats
would add MPs aligned more naturally with a Labour-
Green administration and could make Te Pāti Māori
support or abstention more consequential. A National
overhang would add seats on the right and could improve

the arithmetic for a National-ACT-NZ First government.
Under the latest RNZ-Reid Research projection, for
example, an overhang would arise if National won more
than 32 electorates or Te Pāti Māori won more than two.
This makes the party vote strategically important and
explains the focus on Opportunity’s threshold position.
A vote share just below 5% would produce no list seats
unless the party won an electorate (which based on
limited polling presently seems unlikely). A result just

above 5% would give it a parliamentary caucus large
enough, on current polling, to become the “kingmaker”
and decide which side can form a government. While
the Opportunity Party says that it could work with either
a centre-left or centre-right government, as detailed in
the appendix its broad policy platform might imply that it
more naturally aligns with the centre-left.
What the polls imply.
Kiwipoll’s six-poll tracker, last updated on 20 October,

put Labour on 27.9% and National on 28.7% - a gap
of just 0.8 percentage points. More strikingly, the two
major parties together attracted only 56.6%. The central
message across all polls is therefore fragmentation:
neither major party is close to governing alone, and almost
half of the represented vote is distributed among parties
that would shape the terms of any governing arrangement.
On that average, the balance between the established
blocs is also extremely narrow. The model associated

with the tracker assigned 56 seats to Labour, the
Greens and Te Pāti Māori, and 61 to National, ACT and
New Zealand First. Neither side reached a majority in a
presumed 124-seat parliament. Opportunity’s average
support of 6.2% translated into seven seats, enough to
take either bloc to the 63-seat threshold needed for a
majority. The average therefore implies that the identity
of the largest party may matter less than whether
Opportunity remains above 5% and which grouping it is

prepared to support.
This is not simply a close two-bloc contest. The polling
average also suggests that the minor parties would enter
negotiations with unusually large leverage. The Greens,
ACT and New Zealand First would consequently have
more scope than usual to influence tax, expenditure,
regulation, immigration and resource policy, or the
allocation of Cabinet positions, making it harder to
determine the likely form of a final coalition agreement.

### Page 3

3
Westpac New Zealand Economics
That said, the leverage that these parties can apply on
their major party coalition partner is limited. The Green
Party cannot credibly threaten that it would form an
alternative coalition with the National Party. And neither
could ACT or NZ First credibly threaten to form an
alternative coalition with the Labour Party (while NZ First
has worked with the Labour Party in the past, it has ruled
out doing so on this occasion). Moreover, it is possible

that minor parties would be punished by the electorate
if a failure to form a government necessitated a second
general election, especially if the minor parties were
perceived to have made unreasonable demands or if the
electorate simply refocused on establishing a workable
parliamentary majority.
The Opportunity Party is the pivotal uncertainty because
its average position is above the threshold, but not
far above it. A result modestly below 5%, without an

electorate victory, would remove its votes from the
seat allocation and substantially change the arithmetic,
favouring the centre-right bloc. A result around its recent
6% polling average (or higher) would make the party
central to government formation.
Formally, National has ruled out working with
Opportunity, due to its objection to the latter’s tax policy.
For its part, Opportunity has said that it would talk first to
the party with the most seats, which on present polling

could be either Labour or National. However, as noted
previously, given Opportunity’s policy orientation, it
seems more likely that it would ultimately join a centre-
left coalition than a centre-right coalition.
To summarise, the bloc contest is close, and the route
to a majority depends on threshold and electorate
outcomes that remain uncertain. With that in mind, we
think that the following two scenarios are the most likely
outcomes of the election.

Scenario one: a returned centre-right
government, with greater minor party
representation.
The first scenario is that the existing coalition is returned,
albeit with the minor parties gaining strength and thus
additional leeway to pursue their policy agendas. We
assume that the coalition is not dependent on the
Opportunity Party to govern, which would be more likely
to occur if the latter fell marginally short of the party
vote 5% threshold needed to enter parliament in the

first place.
A renewed National-led government would begin with
considerable continuity. National’s fiscal plan retains
the current coalition’s focus on restoring surplus,
reducing debt as a share of GDP , keeping new spending
constrained and making greater use of planning reform
and private capital. It projects an OBEGALx surplus of
$4 billion in 2028/29, with net core Crown debt peaking
at 43.9% of GDP in 2027/28 and falling to 39.5% by
2030/31. National has ruled out new tax cuts during the

next term, saying tax relief should wait until the books
are back in surplus and surpluses are growing. But a
coalition in which ACT and New Zealand First held a
larger share of seats would be different from the current
more National-dominated administration. Unless its vote
share is particularly low, National would almost certainly
still provide the Prime Minister (sharing the role with NZ
First and ACT in the context of a three-year electoral
term seems quite unlikely – the latter will likely share the

Deputy role as they have in the current term). The three
parties share a common fiscal framework at the macro
level. However, National would have less ability to defer or
dilute its partners’ priorities.
Fiscal policy and the size of government.
ACT would press for a faster and deeper reduction in
the state’s footprint. Its fiscal plan proposes cutting
$9.5bn across the forecast horizon from spending outside
welfare and protected frontline services, reducing future

operating allowances by $400m a year to $2.0bn, and
moving non-welfare spending back towards 2017 real per-
capita levels. ACT says this would lower debt by $12.4bn
by 2031 relative to current forecasts. It also proposes
consolidating government into 19 departments and
18 ministers.
On its own, that agenda would pull a renewed
government beyond National’s more incremental
expenditure control. Coalition negotiations would likely
turn on the pace of implementation and the definition

of protected frontline services. The economic effect
would depend on whether savings represented genuine
efficiency gains or reductions in service capacity and
transfers. In the near term, a faster consolidation could
subtract from demand, while lower debt issuance
and a smaller structural deficit would strengthen the
fiscal buffer.
However, New Zealand First would push in a different
direction. Its programme combines selective tax relief
with active government intervention, regional grants

and strategic ownership. The tension within the coalition
would therefore not simply be between ‘more’ and ‘less’
government. ACT would seek a smaller and more market-
oriented state; New Zealand First would accept a larger
role where it supported national ownership, regions,
families or strategic industries. New Zealand First’s
proposal to reduce the corporate tax rate for SME’s to
20% (from 28%) may well be accepted by its coalition
partners during coalition negotiations – possibly phased

in over time – and would certainly be much cheaper
than the fiscal cost of some other policies promoted
by the party (notably compensating employees for
higher compulsory Kiwisaver contributions and the
nationalisation of the Bank of New Zealand).

### Page 4

4
Westpac New Zealand Economics
Tax, welfare and retirement.
ACT’s longer-run objective is a lower and flatter tax
system. Its immediate plan removes tax on qualifying
KiwiSaver investment earnings, ends the government
KiwiSaver contribution and returns net Emissions Trading
Scheme auction revenue to households through a
carbon-tax refund. Broader tax relief is deferred until
the books are balanced. ACT would raise the NZ Super
eligibility age by three months a year to 67 by 2035, while

allowing compassionate early access for people with long
careers in physically demanding work. It would also pause
contributions to the NZ Super Fund.
On welfare, ACT proposes tighter medical certification
and reassessment for disability-related benefits, a five-
year stand-down before migrants can access welfare,
and lower spending outside protected services. NZ
First would add its own eligibility restrictions, including
limiting NZ Super to citizens from 2029, while proposing

compulsory KiwiSaver enrolment from birth and a Crown
contribution. These policies would create difficult design
questions around transitional arrangements, hardship
and administration, but the broad direction would be
towards tighter eligibility and stronger conditionality.
Regulation, resources and labour markets.
A stronger ACT-NZ First presence would reinforce
deregulation in planning, employment and resource
development. ACT would seek property-rights-based

planning law, easier dismissal, a three-year minimum
wage freeze, a lower training wage for workers under
20 and removal of Te Mana o te Wai from consenting.
It would repeal the Zero Carbon Act. National’s own
RMA replacement and fast-track agenda would provide
substantial common ground.
NZ First would push more directly for regional and
strategic development: a special economic zone at
Marsden Point, offshore oil and gas surveying, expanded
mineral development, and the return of 50% of mining

royalties to producing regions. It also proposes breaking
up the power gentailers and has advocated bringing a
major bank back into New Zealand public ownership.
Both the National Party and NZ First have policies that
aim to force additional competition in the supermarket
sector, but these are opposed by the ACT Party.
Nonetheless, the result could be a government that is
simultaneously more deregulatory in consenting and
more interventionist in ownership and competition policy.

Immigration would also tighten. ACT proposes annually
reviewed work-visa categories, a Rural Workforce Visa,
stronger overstayer enforcement and physical-presence
requirements for permanent residents. NZ First proposes
replacing the Accredited Employer Work Visa with a
shortage-led Skilled Work Visa and holding a referendum
on a 20,000 annual cap on resident visas. National’s more
skills-focused approach would be under pressure from
both partners to place greater weight on enforcement,

settlement obligations and lower aggregate inflows.
Scenario two: Labour and the Greens,
supported by Opportunity.
The second main scenario that we view as likely is a
Labour Party-led coalition with the Green Party, with the
Opportunity Party supporting either inside the coalition
or via a confidence-and-supply arrangement. Depending
on precise outcome of the election, a confidence-and-
supply arrangement may also be required with Te Pāti
Māori to secure the necessary parliamentary majority.

A Labour-led alternative would represent a clearer break
with the current policy direction, but the size of that break
would depend on the success of the minor partners in
embedding their policies in the coalition agreement.
For its part, the Labour Party would aim to restore and
expand selected public services while remaining inside
its published fiscal framework. Its plan retains a $2.4
billion operating allowance for each Budget, targets an
OBEGAL surplus in 2028/29 and seeks to reduce net

debt below 20% of GDP over time. It would introduce
a 28% capital gains tax on post-1 July 2027 gains from
investment residential and commercial property, with
major exemptions, and use the revenue to help fund
health initiatives.
Labour’s central commitments include free prescriptions,
three free doctors’ visits, additional screening and
maternity services, 6,000 additional state houses,
public-transport fare caps, expanded apprenticeships, a
care-worker pay increase and partial reversal of planned

public-service cuts.
It would also restore the Reserve Bank’s dual price-
stability and maximum-sustainable-employment
mandate. As we have discussed in detail in a previous
note, in the current environment – dominated by supply
shocks – the return of the dual mandate could mean that
the RBNZ is prepared to accommodate a slower return of
inflation to target to reduce the impact on employment.
The Greens would shift the centre of gravity.
A strong representation of Green Party ministers in

the Cabinet would seek to move a coalition further on
redistribution, public ownership, housing and climate
policy. The party’s tax programme includes a 2.5% wealth
tax on net assets above $10 million excluding the family
home, a capital-acquisitions tax above $1 million with
exemptions, a 45% top personal rate, a higher company
tax rate for the largest firms, and a levy on large banks.
The stated objective is to raise about $5.1 billion net and
shift the tax burden from wages towards wealth and

large corporations.

### Page 5

5
Westpac New Zealand Economics
These taxes are needed to finance the Green Party’s
extensive promises on spending (see below). Formally,
the Labour Party has explicitly ruled out supporting the
Green Party’s tax policies to secure a Labour Party-led
coalition. Whether that pre-election commitment would
be reversed after the election is a matter of conjecture.
On spending and ownership, the Green Party proposes
free dental care and public transport, stronger income

support, expanded school lunches, more public housing,
a publicly owned renewable-energy entity and the
acquisition of supermarket stores to establish KiwiMart.
A centre-left government, especially with strong Green
Party representation, may find it harder to resist pressure
to lift public service salaries and the minimum wage
in response to fuel-driven inflation. Relative to PREFU
forecasts, this would imply additional expenditure that
would need to be met with either additional sources of

revenue or additional debt issuance.
Labour would resist parts of this agenda on cost,
implementation risk and fiscal credibility. The most likely
coalition bargain would therefore be a narrower set of
high-priority Green Party reforms, financed by some
combination of new revenue, reprioritisation and the
available Budget allowances.
The regulatory direction would be unambiguously
stronger. Labour and the Greens overlap on restoring
pay equity (which was not explicitly budgeted for in

Labour’s fiscal plan), strengthening worker protections,
improving rental standards, expanding public housing
and using government more actively in markets with
weak competition. Climate, freshwater and biodiversity
rules would tighten relative to the current government,
and mining on conservation land would face much
greater barriers.
Opportunity’s role, potentially outside Cabinet.
Opportunity could support a Labour-Green government,
either directly joining the coalition or through a confidence-

and-supply agreement. While the former would give
Opportunity seats at the Cabinet table, the latter
arrangement would let the party retain a distinct identity,
trading reliable support on confidence and budgets for a
defined policy programme. It may also provide more scope
for the party to “learn the ropes” with less public scrutiny,
given that none of its prospective MPs have ever been in
Parliament, let alone held Cabinet positions.
Regardless of what form the arrangement takes,

Opportunity’s landmark policies are neither a simple
extension of Labour nor of the Greens. Opportunity
proposes a “tax reset”, including a land value tax, with a
lower rate for rural land, alongside personal income tax
rates of 28%, 34% and 39%. The revenue raised would be
used to fund a “Citizen’s Income” payment to almost all
people, which would also replace several current social
welfare benefits. That said, Opportunity has conceded
that the land tax and Citizen’s Income are unlikely to be

implemented in the next Parliament given that it will
probably be the smallest of the coalition partners.
In negotiations, Opportunity would press for the
inclusion of its five bottom lines. These are (1) giving
the Commerce Commission more teeth and greater
independence to tackle excessive market power in
supermarkets and other uncompetitive sectors, (2)
regulating lobbying and capping political donations,
(3) developing a 10-year plan to "fix the health system"

alongside health professionals, with clear commitments
on funding, workforce and infrastructure, (4) phasing
out bottom trawling, working with industry, iwi and
coastal communities to fairly manage the transition,
(5) recommitting to Paris Agreement emissions
reduction obligations and protecting the Climate Change
Commission's independence. Opportunity’s other policies
include allowing net debt to exceed 50% of GDP if the
additional borrowing finances productive infrastructure.

It also proposes compulsory KiwiSaver contributions
rising to 6% for employees and employers (a policy it
shares with National), free public transport, stronger
competition enforcement, higher R&D spending and a
ten-year cross-party education plan. In a confidence
and supply arrangement Opportunity could reserve the
right to oppose other legislation it disagrees with, but
the arrangement would need sufficiently clear fiscal
guardrails to avoid repeated uncertainty over Budgets

and concerns about the stability of the Government.
The economic stakes.
The two scenarios imply meaningfully different policy
mixes, but neither government would operate without
constraints. The current fiscal forecasts already assume
a return to surplus and a declining debt ratio later in the
forecast period. Delivery depends on tight expenditure
control, revenue growth and an economic outlook
exposed to oil prices, global interest rates and domestic

productivity. Coalition agreements that materially add
to operating spending or reduce revenue would need
offsetting measures if the party heading the coalition
wished to preserve its stated fiscal track.
For monetary policy, the direct institutional contrast is
Labour’s proposal to restore the Reserve Bank’s dual
mandate. In practice, the larger near-term influence may
come through fiscal demand, labour market regulation,
migration and energy policy. A faster fiscal consolidation

on the right would tend to reduce demand pressure, while
a larger operating spending programme on the left would
increase demand pressure. Infrastructure investment
is somewhat different: if well selected and delivered, it
can lift productive capacity over the medium term, but
it can also add to near-term capacity pressure before
supply benefits arrive. And given the current interest rate
environment, the hurdle that must be crossed to justify
raising debt is higher than it has been for years.

## Document 424: Threats to European diesel imports_ risk of shortages ruled out for the time being, but increased risk of price rises (2026-10-01)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Threats to European diesel imports_ risk of shortages ruled out for the time being, but increased risk of price rises (2026-10-01).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 1st October 2026

EcoFlash

Threats to European diesel imports: risk of shortages ruled out for the time being, but increased 
risk of price rises

In Europe, the possibility of a US ban on diesel exports has heightened concerns about price trends for this widely used 
fuel1, which has already risen significantly since the outbreak of the conflict in Iran. It has become a major political issue 
on both sides of the Atlantic in the run-up to decisive elections.

A particularly tight diesel market due to a shortage of buffer stocks 
Following the outbreak of the conflict, diesel prices did indeed see some of the sharpest rises on the oil market due to particularly 
tight market conditions. Since the end of February, whilst the price of a barrel of crude has risen by 46%, diesel prices have soared 
by 100% in Europe and by 76% in the United States.

The factors alleviating pressure on the refined products market, and more specifically the diesel market, were more limited than in 
the case of crude oil. Shipments passing through the Strait of Hormuz (around 17% of maritime diesel trade prior to the conf lict) 
have fallen by around 75%. Added to this is the halt in Russian exports due to war damage, which is depriving the diesel market of 
around 12% of its maritime exports. Furthermore, unlike with crude oil, China has not acted as a stabilising  force in the diesel 
market. On the contrary, the energy crisis has led to a halt in Chinese exports of refined products (including diesel) to the  rest of 
Asia from April onwards. The resumption of Chinese exports over the last two months remains fragile, given, in particular, that 
diesel stocks are at their lowest level in 15 months.

The United States has eased these tensions in the diesel market by increasing its exports of refined products (up 25% on average 
in the first half of 2026 compared with the average over the last five years). This rise in exports has required high refinery utilisation 
rate (close to maximum capacity) and, to a lesser extent, a reduction in stocks (-11% compared with the average over the last five 
years as at mid -September for middle distillates 2). All these factors have contributed to tightening the US domestic market and 
pushed retail diesel prices to record levels. Road diesel prices (US average) have risen by 68% since the end of February and by 
74% year-on-year.

A structurally high level of vulnerability in Europe… 
Against this market backdrop, characterised by mounting tensions and very limited room for manoeuvre , Europe would be 
particularly hard hit by a reduction in – or even a complete halt to – US exports. According to Eurostat, imports accounted for 46% 
of European demand for diesel in the first half of 2026, with 40% of these imports coming from the United States. Europe, which

1 Diesel accounts for 17% of the primary energy mix in the EU and 7.3% in the United States. 
2 A category of refined petroleum products comprising mainly diesel and aviation fuel.

### Page 2

has lost a great deal of production capacity in recent years, is operating its refineries at near -maximum capacity. Its room for 
manoeuvre is therefore limited.

… But the risk of a shortage has been ruled out in the short term 
The risk of a shortage, however, appears limited in the short term. The situation regarding European stocks is not comfortable, but 
it is not alarming. Commercial diesel stocks in the ARA region 3 are currently at historically low levels (around 12 million barrels), 
23% below their level at the end of September 2025. As for emergency stocks (EU Emergency Stocks), these are, according to the 
European Commission, at a sufficient level. Eurostat estim ates this figure at 270 million barrels in June 2026 (stable from a year 
earlier), representing approximately 60 days’ worth of EU consumption. Europe imports around 0.9 mb/d of diesel from the United 
States, and emergency stocks would therefore cover around 60 days’ consumption at 4.5 mb/d.

The rise in prices could be significant 
Whatever the outcome of the conflict, diesel prices are likely to continue to rise in the short term. According to our base -case 
scenario, crude oil prices are expected to remain very high in Q4 2026 due to ongoing tensions in the Persian Gulf, whilst a return 
to normal levels of diesel exports from Russia and the Middle East appears unlikely by that time.

Among the factors likely to cause future tensions, the start of the maintenance season in Europe could affect production leve ls, 
whilst demand for diesel in the United States is typically high in the autumn (agricultural harvests and pre -winter storage). I n a 
scenario where tensions remain low, the diesel refining margin (crack spread) could rise by 5% in Q4 compared with its level at the 
end of September 2026, before gradually falling back during 2027. An intermediate scenario (a 10% rise in the crack spread in Q4 
2026) would result in moderate constraints on US diesel exports and a drawdown of European stocks. Finally, an adverse scenario 
(a 35% rise in the crack spread in Q4 2026) would imply severe restrictions on US diesel exports and a delayed European response.

What impact will this have on inflation in the euro area? 
According to our estimates, the rise in diesel prices would exacerbate the expected peak in inflation in the euro area without altering 
its timing. In our baseline scenario for September 2026 (see our recently published EcoPerspective), headline inflation would peak 
at 4.1% y/y in January 2027 before gradually falling back towards 2% by the end of the year. The two least adverse scenarios 
would add only a limited amount to the peak: +0.1 pp in the first scenario (+5% for the crack spread in Q4 compared with the end 
of Q3) and +0.2 pp in the intermediate scenario (+10%), with inflation reaching 4.2% and 4.3% respectively in January 2027. 
However, there is an upside risk in the second scenario linked to the uncertain extent of the spillov er effects. In both cases, the 
main impact would be felt through fuel prices (which quickly reflect rising market prices) and would fade over the course of 2027. 
In the case of the adverse scenario (+35%), the additional inflation would be close to 0.6 pp by early 2027, pushing inflation above 
4.6%. A shock of this magnitude would, in fact, reduce businesses’ ability to absorb rising costs, which would lead to price increases 
across a broader range of goods (food, industrial goods). Subsequently, the effect would only fade gradually.

Pascal Devaux 
Economist, Middle East, Western Balkans – Energy 
pascal.devaux@bnpparibas.com

Guillaume Derrien 
Economist, Europe, Eurozone, International Trade 
guillaume.a.derrien@bnpparibas.com

3 Amsterdam-Rotterdam-Antwerp, Europe’s main hub for the storage of raw materials.
